obstruction to the existence of a Griffiths-transverse deformation of the Hodge filtration to the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation in characteristic
p
. The latter will vanish to order
\omega(p)
by the assumption of
(
\omega(p)
-)
integrality, so the former will vanish modulo
p
to order
\omega(p)/p
. As this latter quantity tends to infinity with
p
by definition of
\omega(p)
-integrality, we will see that the obstruction to a Griffiths-transverse deformation of the Hodge filtration vanishes in characteristic zero.

We complete the proof of
Theorem 10.1.3
in
§ 11.2
.

At this point we suggest that the reader interested in the precise details of the proof read the Appendices; for the reader more interested in the general idea, we suggest referring to the summary of
Appendix B
, namely
§ 7.6
, as necessary.

10.2.
Base case of induction

We let
F_{\text{Hodge}}^{\bullet}
denote the Hodge filtration on
\mathscr{E}
, and
\operatorname{gr}_{F^{\bullet}_{\text{Hodge}}}(\mathscr{E})
the associated graded bundle. This bundle is equipped with a Higgs field
\theta
given as the associated graded of
\nabla
, which has degree
-1
by Griffiths transversality. Our goal in this section is to prove:

Lemma 10.2.1
.

We keep the notation of
10.1.1
. After possibly replacing
R
by
R[\frac{1}{f}]
for some
f\in R
, the map

KS:H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{s}}(-\log D_{s}))\overset{H^{1}(\theta)}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}_{\text{Higgs}}(\overline{X}_{s},\operatorname{End}(\operatorname{gr}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}^{\bullet}}({\mathscr{E}}),\theta)/F^{0})

vanishes on the image of the Kodaira-Spencer map
T_{S,s}\rightarrow H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{s}}(-\log D_{s}))
.

We will conclude in
10.2.6
from this (using degeneration of the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence) that the Hodge filtration on
\mathscr{E}
extends Griffiths-transversally to the isomonodromic deformation of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
over the first-order neighborhood of
s
. Strictly speaking this step is contained in the induction step of
§ 10.3
, but it is simpler in a number of ways, so we include it as a warm-up, since
§ 10.3
is mathematically (and notationally) much more involved.

Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a closed point of
\operatorname{Spec}(R)
of residue characteristic
p>0
, and
s_{\mathfrak{p}}
the reduction of
s
modulo
\mathfrak{p}
. We use the subscript
s_{\mathfrak{p}}
to denote objects obtained by basechanging along
s_{\mathfrak{p}}
, so for example
\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is obtained by basechanging
\overline{X}_{s}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(R)
along
s_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proposition 10.2.2
.

We have a natural map of de Rham complexes, induced by the
p
-curvature map
\psi_{p}

F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR}\rightarrow\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}.

where here
F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is given the canonical (Frobenius-pullback) connection.

Moreover, this map is compatible with filtrations
\tau^{i}
and
F^{i}_{conj}
on these complexes, defined as follows:

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdots

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdots

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}:=[F^{*}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\rightarrow F^{*}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\otimes\Omega_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{1}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})\rightarrow\ker(d)\rightarrow\cdots]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle F^{-1}_{conj}\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}

\displaystyle\tau^{0}:=[F^{*}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\rightarrow\ker(d)\rightarrow 0]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle F^{0}_{conj}\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}

\displaystyle\tau^{1}=[\ker(d)\rightarrow 0]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle F^{1}_{conj}\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdots

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \cdots

Proof.

That the map is a map of complexes follows from the fact that the
p
-curvature map

\psi_{p}:F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})\rightarrow\End(\mathscr{E})

is flat
[
Kat70
, 5.2.3]
. That it is compatible with the given filtrations is immediate from the fact that the associated graded of
\operatorname{gr}_{F_{\text{conj}}}\mathscr{E}
has vanishing
p
-curvature.
∎

Definition 10.2.3
.

For the filtered complex
(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR},F_{conj}^{\bullet})
, we denote the terms in the two spectral sequences from
§ 9.2
simply by
\mathscr{E}^{\prime p,q}_{1}
and
E_{2}^{\prime p,q}
, respectively. Similarly, for the filtered complex
(F^{*}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR},\tau^{\bullet})
(with the filtration as in
10.2.2
), we obtain the terms of their respective spectral sequences
\mathscr{E}^{p,q}_{1},E_{2}^{p,q}
.

We now describe these spectral sequences explicitly.

Proposition 10.2.4
.

Let
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
be the pullback of
\mathscr{E}
to the Frobenius twist
\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime}
of
\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

(1)

We have

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\prime p,q}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{gr}^{-p}_{F_{conj}}\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}

\displaystyle E_{2}^{\prime p,q}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime},\operatorname{gr}^{q}_{F_{Hodge}}(\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})_{dR}))

Note that, when
(p,q)=(2,-1)
, we have

E_{2}^{\prime p,q}=\mathbb{H}^{1}(\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime},\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}(\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})_{dR})).

If the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence for
\End{\mathscr{E}}
(where the de Rham complex of the latter is equipped with the Hodge filtration) degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page, then the conjugate spectral sequence

E_{2}^{\prime p,q}\Rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR})

degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page.

(2)

We have

(10.1)

\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p,q}=\begin{cases}T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega^{-p+1}_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\ \ \text{if}\ \ p+q=-p+1,\\
0\ \ \ \ \text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{-(p+k)+1}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})[-(p+k+q)])

\displaystyle=H^{-k}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{-(p+k)+1}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime}),

where
k
is defined by the equality
2(p+k)=1-q
, and our convention is that this group vanishes when
q
is even. Explicitly, we have

E_{2}^{p,q}=H^{-p+(1-q)/2}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{-(p+k)+1}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime}).

Note that, when
(p,q)=(2,-1)
, we have
E_{2}^{p,q}=H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}))
.

(3)

the map

T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})=\mathscr{E}^{1,-1}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\prime 1,-1}=\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})

is given by the Higgs field
\theta^{\prime}
of
\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{Hodge}}\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

This is essentially a routine computation using the definitions in
§ 9.2
, for which we now provide the details.

For (1), we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\prime p,q}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}^{p+q}(\operatorname{gr}^{p}_{conj}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}))

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{gr}^{p}_{conj}(\End(\mathscr{E})))^{\nabla}\otimes\Omega^{p+q}_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime}}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{gr}_{F_{Hodge}}^{-p}\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega^{p+q}_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime}}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})

by Cartier theory, using that the associated graded of the conjugate filtration has zero
p
-curvature, and its Frobenius descent is identified with the associated graded of the Hodge filtration; see
§ 4.2
.

Following
9.2.1
,
E_{2}^{\prime p,q}
is given by the hypercohomology
\mathbb{H}^{p+q}
of the complex

[\cdots\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{-p+1}\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega^{p+q-1}\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{-p}\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega^{p+q}\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{-p-1}\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega^{p+q+1}\rightarrow\cdots]

with the middle term placed in degree
p+q
(here we suppress subscripts and
(\log D)
for space reasons; the associated graded is with respect to the Hodge filtration). By
9.2.2
, the differential is given by the Higgs field
\theta^{\prime}
. This is precisely the complex
\operatorname{gr}^{q}_{F_{Hodge}}(\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})_{dR})
, as claimed, i.e. the
q
-th graded component of the Higgs complex of
\operatorname{gr}_{F_{Hodge}}\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})
, appropriately shifted.

The statement about degeneration of spectral sequences follows from the same statement in
Theorem 4.2.1
.

For (2), by definition, we have
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p,q}=\mathcal{H}^{p+q}(\operatorname{gr}^{p}_{\tau}(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR}))
. From the definition of
\tau
, we see that
\operatorname{gr}^{p}_{\tau}(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR})
has cohomology only in degree
-p+1
, and hence
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p,q}
vanishes unless
p+q=-p+1
. When
p+q=-p+1
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{p+q}(\operatorname{gr}^{p}_{\tau}(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}^{-p+1}(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR})

\displaystyle=T^{\prime}_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})\otimes\Omega^{-p+1}_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}),

as required. Finally, we compute
E_{2}^{p,q}
; this is the hypercohomology of the complex

[\cdots\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p-1,q}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p,q}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{p+1,q}\rightarrow\cdots]

with the middle term placed in degree
p+q
. There is at most one non-zero term in this complex, which is the one in degree
p+k+q
, where
k
satisfies

p+k+q=-(p+k)+1.

In other words,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{-(p+k)+1}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime}[-(p+k+q)])

\displaystyle=H^{-k}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\otimes\Omega_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{-(p+k)+1}(\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime}),

as claimed. Part (3) follows from Katz’s theorem (
Theorem 4.2.1
) comparing the associated graded of the
p
-curvature to the Higgs field
\theta^{\prime}
.
∎

The following is a straightforward consequence of the Griffiths transversality of
F_{Hodge}
on
\mathscr{E}
.

Proposition 10.2.5
.

We have the following natural map of complexes, induced by
T_{\overline{X}_{s}}(-\log D_{s})\rightarrow\gr^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}(\End(\mathscr{E}))
:

[T_{\overline{X}_{s}}(-\log D_{s})\rightarrow 0]\longrightarrow\gr^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}).

The map
KS
of
10.2.1
factors through the induced map
H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{s}}(-\log D_{s}))\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{gr}_{F_{Hodge}}^{-1}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}))
.

Proof of
10.2.1
.

Let
\kappa\in H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{s}}(-\log D_{s}))
be a class in the image of
T_{S,s}
. Let
\kappa_{p}\in H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}))
be the reduction of
\kappa
modulo
\mathfrak{p}
. We will show that
KS(\kappa_{p})=0
for
p
sufficiently large, which will prove the lemma. More precisely, we take
p
large enough so that the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence for
\End(\mathscr{E})
degenerates at the first page, and hence the same is true for the conjugate spectral sequence at the second page. Finally, we may replace
R
with a further finitely-generated localization so that the cohomological condition of
7.5.3
is satisfied, i.e.
H^{1}_{dR}(\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{E}_{0},\nabla_{0}))
is locally free and its formation commutes with arbitrary base change.

Let
Z_{\mathfrak{p}}=F_{\text{abs}}^{-1}(s_{\mathfrak{p}})\subset S_{\mathfrak{p}}
. We also assume that the given (
\omega(p)
-)integral isomonodromic deformation of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
is defined over
Z_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which we may do for
p
sufficiently large by the definition of (
\omega(p)
-)integrality. Let
(\mathscr{G},\nabla)
be the corresponding flat bundle on
(\overline{X},D)_{Z_{\mathfrak{p}}}/Z_{\mathfrak{p}}
. By
B.5.1
, the
p
-curvature of
\mathscr{M}_{dR}(X/S)^{\natural}
vanishes when restricted to
Z_{\mathfrak{p}}
(along the
Z_{\mathfrak{p}}
-point of
\mathscr{M}_{dR}(X/S)^{\natural}
corresponding to
[(\mathscr{G},\nabla)]
). Note that the hypotheses of
B.5.1
are again satisfied by the assumption of (
\omega(p)
-)integrality.
10
10

10

Recall that we choose coordinates
t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S}
on
\widehat{S}
. In applying
B.5.1
we may take
Z=\widehat{R}[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S}]]/(t_{1}^{p},\cdots,t_{\dim S}^{p})
and
m=p\dim S
.

Consider the map on the conjugate spectral sequences (as defined in
9.2.1
) associated with the map of filtered complexes

(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR},\tau^{\bullet})\rightarrow(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR},F^{\bullet}_{conj}).

The terms of this spectral sequence are computed in
10.2.4
; specifically, for
(p,q)=(2,-1)
, we have a map

(10.2)

E_{2}^{2,-1}=H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(\overline{X}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime},\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}(\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})_{dR}))=E_{2}^{\prime 2,-1}.

Note that, by construction, this map is obtained by taking
\mathbb{H}^{1}
of the map of complexes

[\cdots\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{1,-1}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{2,-1}\rightarrow\cdots]\longrightarrow[\cdots\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\prime 1,-1}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\prime 2,-1}\rightarrow\cdots]

which, by
10.2.4
, is precisely the map of complexes

[T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots]\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}(\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})_{dR})

induced by the Higgs field
T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime}).

By our assumption, the spectral sequence
(E_{2}^{\prime p,q})
degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page, and hence
E_{2}^{\prime p,q}=E_{\infty}^{\prime p,q}
for all
p,q
. We claim that the map

(10.3)

E_{\infty}^{2,-1}\rightarrow E_{\infty}^{\prime 2,-1}

vanishes on
\kappa_{p}
. Since the spectral sequence
(E_{2}^{p,q})
also degenerates at the second page, we conclude that
\kappa_{p}
is sent to zero under the map (
10.2
).
By
10.2.5
,
KS
factors through this map, and hence
KS(\kappa_{p})=0
as required.

It remains to show that the map (
10.3
)
vanishes on
\kappa_{p}
. Observe that by
B.4.3
, this map is closely related to the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation, as discussed in the Appendices, as we now explain. The
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation restricted to
s_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\Psi_{p}|_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, is by
B.4.3
precisely

(10.4)

\Psi_{p}|_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}:T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}},s_{\mathfrak{p}}}\to H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\rightarrow H^{1}(F_{\text{abs}}^{*}T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR})\overset{H^{1}(\psi_{p})}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{H}^{1}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR})

where the first map is the Kodaira-Spencer map, the second is induced by the natural inclusion and the third is the one studied in
10.2.2
. By that proposition and degeneration of the conjugate spectral sequence at
E_{2}
, the map (
10.4
) factors through
F^{1}_{conj}\mathbb{H}^{1}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR})
, and the map (
10.3
) is the induced map

\displaystyle H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}))

\displaystyle\overset{\Psi_{p}|_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}}{\longrightarrow}F^{1}_{conj}\mathbb{H}^{1}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR})

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{1}_{F_{conj}}\mathbb{H}^{1}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{gr}^{1}_{F_{conj}}(\End(\mathscr{E})_{dR}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{Hodge}}\End(\mathscr{E}^{\prime}))

It thus suffices to show that the map (
10.4
) vanishes, or in other words that the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation vanishes at
[(\mathscr{E},\nabla)]_{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. But this is immediate from what we arranged at the start of the proof, i.e. that the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation vanishes when restricted to
Z_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
∎

Corollary 10.2.6
.

We keep the notation and assumptions of
10.1.1
. Then the Hodge filtration on
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
extends to a Griffiths-transverse filtration on the isomonodromic deformation of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
to
(\overline{X}_{2,\mathbb{Q}},D_{2,\mathbb{Q}})/S_{2,\mathbb{Q}}
, where, as in
10.1.1
,
S_{n,\mathbb{Q}}
denotes the
n
-order neighborhood of
s_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and
\overline{X}_{2,\mathbb{Q}},D_{2,\mathbb{Q}}
denote the schemes obtained by basechanging
\overline{X},D
to
S_{2,\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proof.

This is immediate from
7.4.1
and the degeneration of the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence, as we now explain. Indeed, we wish to show that the map

T_{S,s}\to H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathbb{Q}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathbb{Q}}}))\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{E})_{dR}/F_{Hodge}^{0})

of
7.4.1
vanishes. This map factors through
F^{-1}_{\text{Hodge}}
, so by degeneration of the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence it suffices to show that the map

T_{S,s}\to H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{s_{\mathbb{Q}}}}(-\log D_{s_{\mathbb{Q}}}))\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{E})_{dR})

induced by the Higgs field vanishes. But this is precisely the content of
10.2.1
.
∎

10.3.
Induction step

We now proceed with the induction step, which is similar to the induction base case, although there are several new complications. Our goal is to extend the Hodge filtration on
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
to a Griffiths-transverse filtration on its isomonodromic deformation to
(\widehat{\overline{X}}_{\mathbb{Q}},\widehat{D}_{\mathbb{Q}})/\widehat{S}_{\mathbb{Q}}
. In this section we will assume we have found such an extension of the Hodge filtration over
S_{n,\mathbb{Q}}
, and handle deformations over split extensions of
S_{n,\mathbb{Q}}
, i.e. extensions of the form
S_{n,\mathbb{Q}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
. We will deduce the analogous extension for all deformations in
§ 11
using some version of the
T^{1}
-lifting theorem.

As in
§ 10.2
, we will need to verify that the obstruction of
7.4.1
vanishes. In
§ 10.2
we did so by comparing it to an associated graded component of the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy filtration, with respect to the conjugate filtration. However, for
n>1
we are not given a conjugate filtration, since we are no longer in the setting of a Picard-Fuchs equation. We will thus have to construct one ourselves. We will do so using Ogus-Vologodsky’s non-abelian Hodge theory, but this will cause an additional complication: it will not be clear anymore that the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation vanishes when restricted to the corresponding section of
\mathscr{M}_{dR}(X/S)^{\natural}
. We will prove this via an argument in mixed characteristic relying on the Higgs-de Rham flow.

Our setup is as follows.

10.3.1.
Setup

Recall that we have a point
s\in S(R)
, and a flat bundle of Picard-Fuchs type
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
on
(\overline{X}_{s},D_{s})
with nilpotent residues along
D_{s}
. We assume in addition that
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
has
\omega(p)
-integral isomonodromic deformation over
\widehat{S}
. Note that (possibly after replacing
R
with some localization) we may assume
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
is semisimple, as it is of Picard-Fuchs type.

The goal of this section is to prove the
“induction step”
, which is recorded in
10.3.11
.

Our induction hypothesis is as follows. We have an affine closed subscheme
\operatorname{Spec}(A)
of
S
with
\operatorname{Spec}(A)_{\text{red}}=s
, such that
the Hodge filtration extends in a Griffiths transverse way to
(\overline{X},D)_{A}
. We denote by
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}})
the flat bundle on
(\overline{X},D)_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}}
which is the isomonodromic deformation of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
. We also assume we are given the Griffiths transverse filtration
F^{\bullet}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}}
on
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}})
extending the given one on
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
. The goal of
10.3.11
is to show that there is no obstruction to further deforming the Hodge filtration (in a Griffiths transverse way) to the isomonodromic deformation to
(\overline{X},D)_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
, where we are given a free
A
-module
M
and a map
{\rm Spec}(A[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\to{}S
.

After replacing
R
with a finitely-generated localization, we may assume
\widehat{S}=\operatorname{Spf}(R[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S}]])
; fix such a set of local coordinates.

The following will be convenient for us:

Assumption 10.3.2
.

In the local coordinates
t_{1},\cdots,t_{n}
chosen above,
A
is cut out by a monomial ideal.

This assumption will be a consequence of the setup of our induction argument in
§ 11
. Note that as
\operatorname{Spec}(A)_{\text{red}}=s
, we have that the ideal of
A
is contains
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S})^{q}
for some
q
.

By our assumption of
\omega(p)
-integrality, we may by localizing
R
assume that all the objects

\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}},F^{\bullet}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}}

descend to
A
, i.e. there are corresponding objects

\mathscr{E}_{A},\nabla_{A},F^{\bullet}_{A}

whose base change along
{\rm Spec}(A_{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A)
are the ones above.
For each maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subset R
, with residue field
k
of characteristic
p
, we have the
k
-algebra

A_{\mathfrak{p}}:=A/\mathfrak{p}.

Base changing along
{\rm Spec}(A_{\mathfrak{p}})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A)
we obtain
(\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{p}},\nabla_{\mathfrak{p}},F^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{p}})
on
\overline{X}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Let
\widehat{R}
,
A_{\widehat{R}}
,
S_{\widehat{R}}
denote the completion of
R
,
A
,
S
at
\mathfrak{p}
, respectively.

Since
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{\mathbb{Q}}
is a semisimple flat bundle, we may write it as

(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{\mathbb{Q}}=\bigoplus_{i}\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{Q},i},

where each
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{Q},i}
is an irreducible flat bundle. As the
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{Q},i}
underly irreducible complex variations of Hodge structure they carry (unique up to reindexing) Griffiths transverse Hodge filtrations
F^{\bullet}_{j,\text{Hodge}}
. We choose this direct sum decomposition so the filtrations on the
\mathscr{F}_{i}
are compatible with the one on
\mathscr{E}
. Explicitly (after possibly replacing
R
with some finitely-generated extension), we may write

(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{\mathbb{Q}}\simeq\bigoplus_{j}\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{Q},j}\otimes V_{j},

where the
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{Q},j}
are mutually non-isomorphic and
\overline{\mathscr{K}}
-simple. (Recall that
\mathscr{K}
is the fraction field of
R
.) Moreover by the theorem of the fixed part, the
V_{j}
carry constant Hodge filtrations making the isomorphism above one of filtered flat bundles; see e.g.
[
LL24
, Proposition 4.1.4(2)]
. Choosing a basis of the
V_{j}
compatible with this filtration yields the desired direct sum decomposition.

Taking the isomonodromic deformations of these flat bundles, we obtain a decomposition

(10.5)

(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathbb{Q}}})=\bigoplus_{i}\mathscr{F}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}},i}.

Replacing
R
by a localization if necessary and applying
7.5.4
, from now on we make the following:

Assumption 10.3.3
.

Each
\mathscr{F}_{A_{\mathbb{Q}},i}
has an integral model
\mathscr{F}_{A,i}
, so that we have a decomposition

(\mathscr{E}_{A},\nabla_{A})=\bigoplus_{i}\mathscr{F}_{A,i},

which is compatible with (
10.5
). Moreover, we assume that
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
satisfies the assumption of
7.5.2
, i.e. that the
R
-module
H^{1}_{dR}(\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{E},\nabla))
is locally free, and its formation commutes with arbitrary base change. Finally, recall that
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
is a Picard-Fuchs equation arising as the cohomology of some smooth projective
f:Y\to X_{s}
; we assume that the modules
\mathscr{H}^{i}_{dR}(Y/X_{s})
are locally free and that their formation commutes with arbitrary base change. Finally we assume that all sufficiently small primes (in terms of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
,
\dim S
, the dimension of
Y/X_{s}
, and the function
g
appearing in the definition of
\omega(p)
-integrality) are invertible in
R
.

Remark 10.3.4
.

The last assumptions of
10.3.3
will allow us to apply Ogus-Vologodsky’s non-abelian Hodge theory and the Fontaine-Laffaille theory of
Theorem 3.2.1
freely. We are always free to replace
R
with a finitely-generated localization in the arguments here so the assumption is harmless.

Finally, we assume:

Assumption 10.3.5
.

Each
\mathscr{F}_{A,i}
is endowed with a Hodge filtration compatible with the Hodge filtration on
(\mathscr{E}_{A},\nabla_{A})
.

This will be automatic from the induction argument of
§ 11
.

Again after replacing
R
by a finitely-generated localization, we may assume it is
\mathbb{Z}
-smooth. Let
{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})=F^{-1}_{\text{abs}}({\rm Spec}(A_{\mathfrak{p}})).
As
S
is smooth, we may fix a lift
F_{0}
of absolute Frobenius
in an affine open of
S_{\widehat{R}}
containing
s_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and let
{\rm Spec}(B_{\widehat{R}})=F^{-1}_{0}(\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\widehat{R}}))
. (Note that despite the notation,
B_{\mathfrak{p}},B_{\widehat{R}}
are not the base-change of some ring
R
to
\mathfrak{p},\widehat{R}
, respectively.)
We let
\widetilde{A},\widetilde{B}
denote the mod
\mathfrak{p}^{2}
reduction of
A,B_{\widehat{R}}
respectively.
The reduction of
F_{0}
mod
\mathfrak{p}^{2}
gives a map
\widetilde{F}:{\rm Spec}(\widetilde{B})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(\widetilde{A})

lifting the Frobenius map
F:{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

The following assumption will be convenient for us to verify the hypotheses of
7.5.3
—in particular,
7.5.1
. Recall
10.3.2
that
{\rm Spec}(A)\subset\hat{S}
is cut out by a monomial ideal in the coordinates
t_{1},\cdots,t_{n}
.

Assumption 10.3.6
.

The map
F_{0}
sends
t_{i}
to
t_{i}^{p}
for all
i
, so that
B_{\widehat{R}}
is also cut out by a monomial ideal.

When we apply our arguments here in
§ 11
, the assumption on
A
will always be satisfied; we choose
F_{0}
now so that
10.3.6
is satisfied.

Definition 10.3.7
.

(1)

We define
\overline{X}_{\tilde{B}}^{\prime}
as the fiber product

(10.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X}_{\widetilde{B}}^{\prime},D_{\widetilde{B}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X}_{\widetilde{A}},D_{\widetilde{A}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(\widetilde{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(\widetilde{A}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{F}}

This is a
W_{2}
-lift of the left square of the diagram

(10.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime},D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},D_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}},D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(A_{\mathfrak{p}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

and therefore
(\overline{X}_{\widetilde{B}}^{\prime},D_{\widetilde{B}}^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(\widetilde{B})
is a
W_{2}
-lift of

(\overline{X}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime},D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
. Note that the bottom horizontal composite map
{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\to{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
is the absolute Frobenius of
{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
, and the bottom right horizontal arrow is the natural closed embedding.

(2)

Using the
W_{2}
-lift
\overline{X}_{\widetilde{B}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(\widetilde{B})
as above,
we define the following connection on
\overline{X}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}
:

(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)\vcentcolon=C^{-1}(G^{*}(\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}))),

where
C^{-1}
is the inverse Cartier transform of
Theorem 9.1.2
.
The decreasing filtration on
(\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}))
by the sub-Higgs bundles

(\mathscr{G}_{m},\theta_{m})=\left(\bigoplus_{i\leq-m}\operatorname{gr}^{i}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})\right)

gives rise (by applying
C^{-1}
to
G^{*}(\mathscr{F}_{m},\theta_{m})
) to a

\nabla
-stable decreasing filtration on
\mathscr{E}_{B}
, which we denote by
F_{conj}
.

Our goal is to consider deformations of
\overline{X}_{A}
to
A[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
, and to compute the obstruction to deform the Hodge filtration on
\mathscr{E}_{A}
. We will compare it to a certain graded component of the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation on the leaf associated to
(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)
, with respect to the conjugate filtration defined above.

Definition 10.3.8
.

(1)

Equip
F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})
with its canonical (Frobenius pullback) connection. We may filter its de Rham complex as in
10.2.2
with the canonical filtration, thus obtaining the filtered complex

(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR},\tau).

(2)

Consider the complex
\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})_{dR}
. The flat bundle
\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})
carries a conjugate filtration induced from the conjugate filtration on
\mathscr{E}_{B}
defined in
10.3.7
.

This in turn defines the filtered complex
(\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})_{dR},F_{conj})
.

As in
10.2.2
, the
p
-curvature map

\psi_{p}:F_{\text{abs}}^{*}T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})\to\operatorname{End}(\mathscr{E}_{B})

induces a map of filtered complexes

(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR},\tau)\rightarrow(\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})_{dR},F_{\text{conj}}).

Lemma 10.3.9

(Key lemma)
.

We maintain notation as in
§ 10.3.1
.

(1)

There is an isomorphism
(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)|_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})
. That is, in the language of
[
OV07
, Definition 4.16]
,
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})
equipped with its Hodge filtration is a Fontaine module; in the language of
[
LSZ19
]
it is a Higgs-de Rham fixed point.

(2)

Moreover the restriction of the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation on
\mathscr{M}_{dR}(X/S)^{\natural}
to the
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-point of
\mathscr{M}_{dR}(X/S)^{\natural}
corresponding to
[(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)]
vanishes. More precisely, the map

(10.8)

H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}))_{dR})\overset{\psi_{p}}{\to}\mathbb{H}^{1}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})_{dR})

vanishes on the image of the composition

(10.9)

F_{\text{abs}}^{*}T_{S,B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\rightarrow F^{*}_{\text{abs}}H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\to H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})).

Here the first map in (
10.8
) is given by the natural inclusion of
T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})
into the de Rham complex of
F^{*}_{\text{abs}}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}))
(namely, the inclusion of flat sections), and the second is induced by the
p
-curvature of
(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)
. The first map in (
10.9
) is the Frobenius pullback of the Kodaira-Spencer map, and the second is the base change morphism.

We postpone the proof of
10.3.9
to the next section; it is the source of the vanishing we use to see that deforming the Hodge filtration is unobstructed.

Proposition 10.3.10
.

(1)

The terms of the spectral sequences for the filtered complexes

(F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR},\tau),(\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})_{dR},F_{\text{conj}})

are
exactly as in
10.2.4
. Precisely,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{\prime p,q}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(G^{*}\operatorname{gr}^{q}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})_{dR}))

\displaystyle E_{2}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=H^{-k}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})\otimes\Omega_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-(p+k)+1}(\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})),

with
k
defined by
2(p+k)=1-q
, and our convention is that the latter group is zero when
k
is not an integer.

(2)

We have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{2,-1}

\displaystyle=H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}))

\displaystyle=(H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}/A_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\otimes_{A_{\mathfrak{p}},F}B_{\mathfrak{p}}

where we denote by
F:A_{\mathfrak{p}}\rightarrow B_{\mathfrak{p}}
the ring map corresponding to the map of schemes

F:{\rm Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A_{\mathfrak{p}})
of
10.3.7
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{\prime 2,-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{H}^{1}(G^{*}\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR})\otimes_{A_{\mathfrak{p}},F}B_{\mathfrak{p}}.

The induced map
E_{2}^{2,-1}\rightarrow E_{2}^{\prime 2,-1}
, which is equivalently a map

H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}/A_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\otimes_{A_{\mathfrak{p}},F}B_{\mathfrak{p}}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR}))\otimes_{A_{\mathfrak{p}},F}B_{\mathfrak{p}},

is given by
\mu\otimes_{A,F}\rm{id}_{B}
, where
\mu
is the map

(10.10)

\mu:H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}/A_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR}))

obtained by taking
\mathbb{H}^{1}
of the map of complexes

[T_{\overline{X}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}/A_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots]\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR})

induced by the Higgs field on
\operatorname{gr}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})
, namely
\operatorname{gr}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})
.

Proof.

Part (
1
) is exactly the same as
10.2.4
, so it remains to
prove (
2
). Note that
F:{\rm Spec}(B)\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A)
is flat, being a basechange of Frobenius on
S
, which is flat on a smooth scheme. The formulas for
E_{2}^{2,-1},E_{2}^{\prime 2,-1}
follow immediately
from (
1
) and flat basechange.

The final claim follows immediately from basechange and
the statement that the induced map

E_{2}^{2,-1}=H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(G^{*}\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR}))=E_{2}^{\prime 2,-1}

is induced by the map of complexes

[T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}})\rightarrow 0\cdots]\rightarrow G^{*}\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR})

given by the Higgs field on
\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. This latter statement follows from
9.1.3
, which identifies the Higgs field on the associated graded with the flat section corresponding to the
p
-curvature defining our map of complexes.

∎

Lemma 10.3.11

(Vanishing of obstruction to deforming in a Griffiths-transverse manner)
.

After possibly replacing
R
by
R[\frac{1}{f}]
for some
f\in R
, map

KS:H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{A}/A}(-\log D_{A}))\rightarrow{H}^{1}_{\text{Higgs}}(\End(\operatorname{gr}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\mathscr{E}_{A},\theta)/F^{0}_{\text{Hodge}})

vanishes on the image of the Kodaira-Spencer map
T_{S,A}\to H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{A}/A}(-\log D_{A}))
.

Proof.

The argument is almost identical to that of
10.2.1
, though we provide some details here for the reader’s convenience. It suffices to show the map vanishes mod
\mathfrak{p}
for
\mathfrak{p}
in an open subset of
\operatorname{Spec}(R)
.

Since the
p
-curvature map

F^{*}_{\text{abs}}T_{S,B_{\mathfrak{p}}}\rightarrow H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D^{\prime}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}))\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{B})_{dR})

vanishes by
10.3.9

the same spectral sequence argument as in the proof of
10.2.1

implies
E_{2}^{2,-1}\rightarrow E_{2}^{\prime 2,-1}
vanishes on the image of

T_{S,B}
.

By
10.3.10
(
2
), this is precisely the map

\mu\otimes_{A,F}\id_{B}
, where
\mu
is the map on
H^{1}
induced by

[T_{\overline{X}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}/A_{\mathfrak{p}}}(-\log D_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots]\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{-1}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\End(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})_{dR})

Since the map
A_{\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{F}B_{\mathfrak{p}}
makes
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
into a faithfully flat
A_{\mathfrak{p}}
-algebra, we deduce
that
\mu
vanishes on the image of
H^{1}(T_{S,A_{\mathfrak{p}}})
; since
KS
factors through
\mu
,
we conclude that
KS
also vanishes on the image of
H^{1}(T_{S,A_{\mathfrak{p}}})
, as required.
∎

Corollary 10.3.12
.

Under the setup of
§ 10.3.1
, suppose the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence for
(\mathscr{F}_{A,i},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
degenerates at
E_{1}
. Recall that
\mathscr{K}=\operatorname{Frac}(R)
.

Then for any
{\rm Spec}(A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\rightarrow S
deforming the given map
{\rm Spec}(A_{\mathscr{K}})\rightarrow S
, the obstruction to deforming
F^{\bullet}
in a Griffiths-transverse manner to the isomonodromic deformation of
(\mathscr{F}_{A_{\mathscr{K}},i},\nabla)
to
(\overline{X}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},D_{A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}})/\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
vanishes.

Proof.

The argument is the same as the argument for
10.2.6
.

Since the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence for
(\mathscr{F}_{A_{\mathscr{K}},i},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
degenerates at
E_{1}
, by
7.4.1
, the obstruction to deforming
(\mathscr{F}_{A_{\mathscr{K}},i},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
isomonodromically and Griffiths-transversally to
(\overline{X}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},D_{A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}})/\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
vanishes if and only if the image of the class in
H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}/A_{\mathscr{K}}}(-\log D_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}))
corresponding to
(\overline{X}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},D_{A_{\mathscr{K}}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}})
under the map

KS_{M}:H^{1}(T_{\overline{X}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}/A_{\mathscr{K}}}(-\log D_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}))\rightarrow{H}^{1}_{\text{Higgs}}(\End(\operatorname{gr}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\mathscr{F}_{A_{\mathscr{K}},i},\theta)/F^{0}_{\text{Hodge}})

vanishes.
But this is a component of the map in
10.3.11
which itself vanishes, as required.
∎

10.4.
Proof of the key lemma

We now begin preparations for the proof of
10.3.9
. This will be a mixed-characteristic argument. The idea will be to lift the construction of
(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)
from the previous section to characteristic zero using the Higgs-de Rham flow of Lan-Sheng-Zuo, giving rise to a bundle
(\mathscr{E}_{B_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla)
on
(\overline{X},D)_{B_{\widehat{R}}}
. We will argue that this bundle is isomonodromic and then use the
1
-periodicity of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
under the Higgs-de Rham flow (or in other words, the fact that it is a Fontaine module) to conclude that it is in fact an isomonodromic deformation of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
. It will then follow from
7.5.3
that
(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)
is itself
1
-periodic, i.e. a Fontaine module. Now the vanishing of the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation will follow from the existence of an (
\omega(p)
)-integral isomonodromic deformation extending
(\mathscr{E}_{B_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla)
by
A.3.4
, the analogue of
2.4.4
for a crystal of functors.

We keep the setup as in
§ 10.3.1
, so that we have the diagram

(10.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})_{B_{\widehat{R}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X},D)_{A_{\widehat{R}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X},D)_{B_{\widehat{R}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\widehat{R}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(A_{\widehat{R}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\widehat{R}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}

Recall that, by the induction hypothesis, we have a filtered
flat bundle
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
such that

•

F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet}
is Griffiths transverse, and

•

(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is isomonodromic.

We now explain how to construct a lift of
(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)
to
(\overline{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})_{B_{\widehat{R}}}
, following ideas of
[
EG25
]
and
[
LSZ19
]
.

Construction 10.4.1
.

Consider the filtered connection

G_{0}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
. As in

[
EG25
, Definition 3.26]
, we can construct the
Artin–Rees bundle
\mathsf{AR}
of

G_{0}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
.

More precisely, this is the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\widehat{R}}}}
-sub-modules

\sum_{i}G_{0}^{*}F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{i}\otimes p^{-i}\subset G_{0}^{*}\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}.

Moreover,
\mathsf{AR}
is equipped with its
natural
p
-connection, coming from the connection
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})
, which we denote by
\nabla_{p}:=p\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}}
.
More precisely, this is a map

\nabla_{p}:\mathsf{AR}\rightarrow\mathsf{AR}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}^{\prime}_{B_{\widehat{R}}}}(\log D_{B_{\widehat{R}}}),

satisfying the
p
-Leibniz rule (see
[
EG25
, Definition 3.7]
) and squaring to zero.

Construction 10.4.2
.

We say that a formal affine open subscheme

\iota:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\overline{X}_{B_{\widehat{R}}}

has a

decent
Frobenius lift, if it
is equipped with a lift
F_{\mathcal{U}}

of absolute Frobenius such
that

(1)

the square

(10.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\widehat{R}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\widehat{R}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{U}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}

commutes, and

(2)

F_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}}(-{D}_{\mathcal{U}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}}(-p{D}_{\mathcal{U}})
, where
{D}_{\mathcal{U}}
is the pullback of
{D}
to
\mathcal{U}
.

For
\mathcal{U}
equipped with such a decent Frobenius lift,
we may form the diagram

(10.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\widehat{R}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(B_{\widehat{R}}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{U}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}

with the square being cartesian.

Remark 10.4.3
.

Note that the data of a decent Frobenius lift is
equivalent to that of a morphism of
B_{\widehat{R}}
-schemes

\alpha:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
lifting the
relative Frobenius map; in the following, when we say a decent Frobenius lift, we will mean either
F_{\mathcal{U}}
or
\alpha
, depending on which is more convenient for the situation.

We can form the connection on the sheaf
\alpha^{*}\mathsf{AR}
by the following recipe: for a section
s
of
\alpha^{*}\mathsf{AR}|_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}
, we set

\nabla_{\mathcal{U},\alpha}(s):=\frac{\mathrm{id}\otimes\alpha^{*}}{p}\nabla_{p}(s).

Lemma 10.4.4
.

We can cover (the formal scheme)
\overline{X}_{B_{\widehat{R}}}
by decent
affine formal open subschemes, and
the connections
(\alpha^{*}\mathsf{AR},\nabla_{\mathcal{U},\alpha})
glue to
a connection on
(\overline{X}_{B_{\widehat{R}}},D_{B_{\widehat{R}}})/B_{\widehat{R}}
.

Proof.

The first part follows immediately from iterating

[
EV92
, Corollary 9.12]
, i.e. first
applying loc.cit. to construct liftings to
\hat{B}^{int}/p^{2}
, then to

\hat{B}^{int}/p^{3}
, and so on.

For the gluing, for a formal affine open

\mathcal{U}\subset\overline{X}_{B_{\widehat{R}}}
and two decent Frobenius liftings

\alpha,\beta:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}^{\prime},

we must provide an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}}
-linear isomorphism

(10.14)

\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}:\alpha^{*}\mathsf{AR}\xrightarrow{\sim}\beta^{*}\mathsf{AR}.

Let us pick étale affine coordinates (shrinking
\mathcal{U}
if necessary)

\tau:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{B_{\widehat{R}}}
; and let
\alpha_{j}
denote the pullback of the coordinate function
t_{j}
under the composition

\mathcal{U}\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\tau^{\prime}}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{B_{\widehat{R}}},

with the last map being the basechange of
\tau
along
F_{0}
. We define the
\beta_{j}
’s similarly.
Following
[
EG25
, Definition 3.27]
, we now define

\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}
by

(s)_{i}\otimes 1\mapsto\sum_{|\underline{i}|\leq i}(\partial^{\underline{i}}(s))_{i-|\underline{i}|}\otimes\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\alpha_{j}-\beta_{j})^{i_{j}}}{p^{|\underline{i}|}\underline{i}!}+\sum_{|\underline{i}|>i}p^{|\underline{i}|-i}(\partial^{\underline{i}}(s))_{0}\otimes\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\alpha_{j}-\beta_{j})^{i_{j}}}{p^{|\underline{i}|}\underline{i}!}.

It is straightforward to check that the

\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}
’s satisfy the cocycle condition, i.e. for
another
\gamma
lifting relative Frobenius, we have

\epsilon_{\beta\gamma}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}=\epsilon_{\alpha\gamma}.

It remains to check that, in (
10.14
),

\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}
is flat, namely that the following

(10.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}\mathsf{AR}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{*}\mathsf{AR}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}\mathsf{AR}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{U}/B_{\widehat{R}}}(\log D_{\mathcal{U}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{*}\mathsf{AR}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{U}/B_{\widehat{R}}}(\log D_{\mathcal{U}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\mathcal{U},\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\mathcal{U},\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}\otimes\mathrm{id}}

commutes; this is a straightforward computation.
∎

Definition 10.4.5
.

We denote by
\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
the bundle with flat connection on
(\overline{X}_{B_{\widehat{R}}},D_{B_{\widehat{R}}})/B_{\widehat{R}}

obtained in
10.4.4
; following
[
EG25
]
we refer to
\Phi
as the
flow functor
.

Proposition 10.4.6
.

The flat bundle
\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
restricted to
\overline{X}_{B_{\mathfrak{p}}}/B_{\mathfrak{p}}
is
precisely the bundle

(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)\vcentcolon=C^{-1}(G^{*}(\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\operatorname{gr}^{\bullet}_{F_{\text{Hodge}}}(\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}))),

of
10.3.7
.

Proof.

This is
[
EG25
, Prop 3.28]
when
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a field. Their proof relies on
[
LSZ15
]
, which is only written in this generality, but all the proofs work verbatim over more general bases.
∎

The key point is now:

Proposition 10.4.7
.

The flat bundle
\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
satisfies the following:

(1)

There is an isomorphism
\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})|_{s_{\widehat{R}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{s_{\widehat{R}}}
.

(2)

\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is isomonodromic.

Proof.

Part (1) is precisely the part of
Theorem 3.2.1
about Fontaine-Laffaille modules; in other words it is the statement that Picard-Fuchs equations are
1
-periodic under the Higgs-de Rham flow (here we use
10.3.3
). See
[
Fal89
, Theorem 6.2]
for a classical reference or e.g.
[
KS20
, Theorem 1.3]
for a reference in the language of Higgs-de Rham flows (in a slightly more general, parabolic, setting, though only in the case of curves—which suffices for our applications).

For part (2), we use
[
EG25
, Proposition 3.39]
, namely, that
\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
is an integral model of the “Frobenius pullback”
F^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})
of
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})
, which is crucially a construction that does not depend on
F^{\bullet}_{A_{\widehat{R}}}
. Namely, we have an isomonodromic flat bundle
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})
on
(\overline{X},D)_{A_{\widehat{R}}}/A_{\widehat{R}}
, by assumption. As
F_{0}
factors through
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{\widehat{R}})
, for each
\mathcal{U}
we have that the map
F_{\mathcal{U}}
chosen in
10.4.2
factors through
(\overline{X},D)_{A_{\widehat{R}}}
, and thus it makes sense to consider
F_{\mathcal{U}}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})
. We claim these bundles glue to an isomonodromic bundle. They are glued via the formula of
[
EG25
, Definition 3.1]
(namely, Taylor’s formula—see
[
EG25
, Proposition 3.3]
for the logarithmic case) giving rise to a flat bundle on
(\overline{X}_{B_{\widehat{R}}},D_{B_{\widehat{R}}})/B_{\widehat{R}}
. Finally, we check that the connection is isomonodromic in characteristic zero, i.e. that
F^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is the restriction of an isomonodromic flat bundle on
(\overline{X},D)
on the formal completion of
S_{\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{Q}}}
at
s_{\widehat{R}_{\mathbb{Q}}}
. We do this in
10.4.8
below, where the isomonodromy assumption is satisfied as
(\mathscr{E}_{\widehat{R}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is isomonodromic by the induction hypothesis, and
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}
as
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
is a Picard-Fuchs equation, hence gives rise to an
F
-isocrystal, see
(or by part (1) and
[
EG25
, Proposition 3.39]
).
∎

Lemma 10.4.8
.

Let
k
be a finite field of characteristic
p>2
and
S=\operatorname{Spec}(W(k)[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{d}]]
, and let
0\in S
be the
W(k)
-point defined by
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{d})
. Let
\overline{X}
be a smooth projective formal
S
-scheme and
D\subset\overline{X}
a simple normal crossings divisor over
S
. Let
Z\subset S
be a closed subscheme with
Z_{\text{red}}=\operatorname{Spec}(W(k))
. Let
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
be a flat bundle on
(\overline{X},D)_{Z}/Z
with
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{Z_{\mathbb{Q}}}
isomonodromic.

Choose the Frobenius lift
F_{S}
on
W(k)[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{d}]]
defined by
t_{i}\mapsto t_{i}^{p}
and let
F^{*}
denote the “Frobenius pullback” used in the proof of
10.4.7
, defined as in
[
EG25
, Proposition 3.3]
; this is a functor from flat bundles on
(\overline{X},D)_{Z}/Z
to flat bundles on
(\overline{X},D)_{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)}/F_{S}^{-1}(Z)
. Suppose
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}
. Then
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)_{\mathbb{Q}}}
is isomonodromic.

Proof.

This is “formal” from the crystalline description of the Frobenius pullback via the functoriality of the crystalline site (which is made explicit via Taylor series in
[
EG25
]
or
[
Ber00
]
); this is the statement that Frobenius pullback is flat for the non-abelian Gauss-Manin connection, and is the non-abelian analogue of the flatness of the crystalline Frobenius
\Phi
of
. In fact, the condition that
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{X_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{X_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}
is unnecessary. However, we give an explicit proof under this assumption, as we found it somewhat illuminating.

Let
\widehat{S}:=\operatorname{Spf}(W(k)[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{d}]])
and let
(\widehat{\overline{X}},\widehat{D})
be the base change of
(\overline{X},D)
to
\widehat{S}
. As above, choose a cover of
\widehat{\overline{X}}
by formal affines equipped with decent Frobenius liftings over
F_{S}
, and étale affine coordinates, so that we may define a Frobenius pullback functor on flat bundles,
F^{*}
, as in the proof of
10.4.7
above or as in
[
EG25
, Proposition 3.3]
.

Let
\widehat{S}_{\mathbb{Q}}=\operatorname{Spf}(\operatorname{Frac}(W(k))[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{d}]])
, and let
\widehat{\overline{X}}_{\mathbb{Q}},\widehat{D}_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the base change of
\overline{X},D
to
\widehat{S}_{\mathbb{Q}}
. We will show that
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{\mathbb{Q}}
is the restriction of a flat bundle on
(\widehat{\overline{X}},\widehat{D})_{\mathbb{Q}}
equipped with an
absolute
connection, and is hence isomonodromic in the sense of
7.1.1
.

Let
S_{n}=(F_{S}^{n})^{-1}(0)
, i.e the subscheme cut out by
(t_{1}^{p^{n}},\cdots,t_{d}^{p^{n}})
. Let
(\overline{X},D)_{n}=(\overline{X},D)|_{S_{n}}
. Note that for
N
sufficiently large,
(F_{S}|_{S_{n}})^{N}
factors through
S_{0}=0
. Hence
(F)^{N*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{n}}
depends only on
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0}}
and carries an absolute connection (as it is pulled back from
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0}}
, which carries an absolute connection). We claim these connections glue on tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}
; we will glue them using the formulae in
7.1.3
. Note that all of these connections are isomorphic when restricted to
(\overline{X}_{0},D_{0})_{\mathbb{Q}}
, by the assumption that
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{X_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{X_{0,\mathbb{Q}}}
. For each
n
, choose an isomorphism
(F)^{N*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0}}\simeq(F)^{(N-1)*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0}}
.

The sheaf
\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}_{n,\mathbb{Q}}/W(k)}(\log D_{n})
is not locally free, so we cannot apply these formulae directly. However,
\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}_{n,\mathbb{Q}}/W(k)}(\log D_{n})|_{\overline{X}_{n-1}}
is locally free, and restricting the absolute connection
(F^{N})^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
on
(\overline{X}_{n},D_{n})
to
(\overline{X}_{n-1},D_{n-1})
yields an “absolute connection”:

\nabla:(F^{N})^{*}\mathscr{E}\to(F^{N})^{*}(\mathscr{E})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}_{n,\mathbb{Q}}/W(k)}(\log D_{n})|_{\overline{X}_{n-1}},

so, for example, it makes sense to apply the operator
\nabla_{\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{j}}}
(where
\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{j}}
is dual to
dt_{j}
) to a section to
(F^{N})^{*}\mathscr{E}
.

This suffices to apply the formulae of
7.1.3
after tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}
; as all the
(F)^{N*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0}}
are identified, this yields an absolute connection
(\mathscr{G},\nabla)
on
(\widehat{\overline{X}},\widehat{D})_{\mathbb{Q}}
as desired. It remains to identify its restriction to
F_{S}^{-1}(Z)_{\mathbb{Q}}
with
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)_{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

But note that the restriction of
(\mathscr{G},\nabla)
to
(\overline{X},D)_{Z_{\mathbb{Q}}}/Z_{\mathbb{Q}}
must agree with
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{Z_{\mathbb{Q}}}
, as both are isomonodromic. But for all sufficiently large
N
,
F^{N*}((\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0}})|_{(\overline{X}_{Z},D_{Z})/Z}
is an integral model of

(\mathscr{G},\nabla)|_{(\overline{X}_{Z},D_{Z})/Z}=(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{Z_{\mathbb{Q}}}.

It follows that
F^{(N+1)*}((\mathscr{E},\nabla)|_{\overline{X}_{0}})|_{(\overline{X}_{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)},D_{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)})/{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)}}
is an integral model of
F^{*}(\mathscr{E},\nabla)_{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)_{\mathbb{Q}}}
; but it is also an integral model of
(\mathscr{G},\nabla)|_{F_{S}^{-1}(Z)_{\mathbb{Q}}}
, completing the proof.

∎

We now proceed with the proof of
10.3.9
.

Proof of
10.3.9
.

Our setup is as in
§ 10.3.1
.

We first check that
(\mathscr{E}_{B},\nabla)|_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}})
. It suffices by
10.4.6
to show the stronger statement that

\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})|_{A_{\widehat{R}}}\simeq(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}}).

By
10.4.7
, these two bundles are isomorphic when restricted to
s_{\widehat{R}}
. But both are isomonodromic in characteristic zero, whence by
10.3.3
and
7.5.3
, they are in fact isomorphic.

Finally, we check the vanishing of the
p
-curvature of the isomonodromy foliation. on restriction to our given
B_{\mathfrak{p}}
-point of
\mathscr{M}_{dR}(X/S)^{\natural}
. By
10.4.7
,
\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is isomonodromic over
B_{\widehat{R},\mathbb{Q}}
.

Recall (from
10.3.2
) that we have chosen coordinates
t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S}
on
\widehat{S}
, and that the ideal cutting out
A
is monomial with respect to these coordinates, say the ideal
(t_{1}^{A_{1}},\cdots,t_{\dim S}^{A_{\dim S}})
where
A_{i}
are multi-indices. We moreover have some
q
such that

(t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S})^{q}\subset(t_{1}^{A_{1}},\cdots,t_{\dim S}^{A_{\dim S}}).

Let
Z
be the subscheme of
S_{\widehat{R}}
cut out by
(t_{1}^{pA_{1}},\cdots,t_{\dim S}^{pA_{\dim S}})
;
Z
is a lift of
\operatorname{Spec}(B_{\mathfrak{p}})
. As in the setup for
B.5.1
, let
Z_{+}
be the subscheme cut out by

(t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S})^{m+p+1},

where
m
is minimal such that
Z\subset V((t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S})^{m})
; for example, we may take
m=pq\dim S
. By the assumption of
\omega(p)
-integrality, we have a model
(\mathscr{G},\nabla)
of our isomonodromic deformation of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
over
Z_{+}
.

Again by
7.5.3
,
\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})
must agree with
(\mathscr{G},\nabla)_{B_{\widehat{R}}}
. Thus the hypotheses of
B.5.1
are satisfied and the
p
-curvature vanishes as desired.
∎

Remark 10.4.9
.

Instead of using the uniqueness of integral isomonodromic deformations under suitable conditions—
7.5.3
—we could also have argued via polystability of the special fibers of the bundles

\Phi(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}},F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}^{\bullet})\simeq(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\widehat{R}}},\nabla_{A_{\widehat{R}}}),

which will hold on an open of
\operatorname{Spec}(R)
.

11.
Smoothness of deformation space

We now prepare to prove
Theorem 10.1.3
.

11.1.
T^{1}
-lifting theorem

Let us formulate a version of the
T^{1}
-lifting theorem suitable for our induction argument. Let
\mathscr{K}
be a field of characteristic zero,
\mathfrak{R}=\mathscr{K}[[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}]]
with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})
,
I\subset\mathfrak{R}
an ideal, and
\mathfrak{T}_{i}=\mathfrak{R}/I
. Let
\mathfrak{R}_{n}=\mathfrak{R}/\mathfrak{m}^{n}
. For
B
a ring and
M
a
B
-module, we denote by
B\oplus M
the ring whose underlying abelian group is the direct sum
B\oplus M
, with multiplication defined by

(b_{1},m_{1})\cdot(b_{2},m_{2})=(b_{1}b_{2},b_{1}m_{2}+b_{2}m_{1}).

Lemma 11.1.1
.

Suppose that
I\subset\mathfrak{m}^{n}
. Suppose that for all free finite rank
\mathfrak{R}_{n}
-modules
M
, and all maps
f:\mathfrak{R}\to\mathfrak{R}_{n}\oplus M
such that the composition
\mathfrak{R}\xrightarrow{f}\mathfrak{R}_{n}\oplus M\to\mathfrak{R}_{n}
is the natural quotient map

q_{n}:\mathfrak{R}\to\mathfrak{R}/\mathfrak{m}^{n},

we have that
f(I)=0
, i.e.
f
descends to a (unique) map
\mathfrak{T}_{i}\to\mathfrak{R}_{n}\oplus M
. Then
I\subset\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}
.

Proof.

We set
M=\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{R}/\mathscr{K}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{R}}\mathfrak{R}_{n}
to be the restriction of the (completed) Kähler differentials of
\mathfrak{R}/\mathscr{K}
to
\mathfrak{R}_{n}
, so that
M=\oplus_{i}\mathfrak{R}_{n}dx_{i}
. Let
d_{n}
be the composition

d_{n}:\mathfrak{R}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{R}/\mathscr{K}}\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{R}/\mathscr{K}}\otimes_{\mathfrak{R}}\mathfrak{R}_{n}=M,

where
d
is the exterior derivative and the second map above is the natural quotient map, and let
f:\mathfrak{R}\to\mathfrak{R}_{n}\oplus M
be the map
(q_{n},d_{n}).
Note that this is a ring homomorphism, as

f(ab)=(q_{n}(a)q_{n}(b),d_{n}(ab))=(q_{n}(a)q_{n}(b),q_{n}(a)d_{n}(b)+q_{n}(b)d_{n}(a)).

By assumption,
f(I)=0
, i.e.
d_{n}(g)=0
for all
g
in
I
. Fixing
g\in I
, we thus have

dg=\sum_{i}\frac{\partial g}{\partial x_{i}}dx_{i}

where
\frac{\partial g}{\partial x_{i}}\in\mathfrak{m}^{n}
for all
i
. But this implies
g
is in
\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}
, using that
\mathscr{K}
has characteristic zero. As
g
in
I
was arbitrary, this completes the proof.
∎

It is immediate that it suffices to check the condition of
11.1.1
for rank one free
\mathfrak{R}_{n}
-modules:

Corollary 11.1.2
.

Suppose that
I\subset\mathfrak{m}^{n}
. Suppose that for all maps
f:\mathfrak{R}\to\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
such that the composition
\mathfrak{R}\xrightarrow{f}\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}\to\mathfrak{R}_{n}
is the natural quotient map

q_{n}:\mathfrak{R}\to\mathfrak{R}/\mathfrak{m}^{n},

we have that
f(I)=0
, i.e.
f
descends to a (unique) map
\mathfrak{T}_{i}\to\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
. Then
I\subset\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}
.

11.2.
Deforming the Hodge filtration—last step

We maintain the notation and assumptions as in
§ 10.1
. Since
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
is semisimple, we may write it as

(\mathscr{E},\nabla)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}(\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla)

with each
\mathscr{F}_{i}
being irreducible. Each
(\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla)
is equipped with a Hodge filtration, which we denote simply by
F^{\bullet}
when the context is clear. We arrange the direct sum decomposition so that the Hodge filtrations on the
\mathscr{F}_{i}
are compatible with that on
\mathscr{E}
, as in
§ 10.3.1
.

Definition 11.2.1
.

For each
i
, we define the deformation problem
\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F}
, where for an Artin local
\mathscr{K}
-algebra
A_{\mathscr{K}}
,
\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F}(A_{\mathscr{K}})
is the set of isomorphism classes of tuples

(s_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},\mathscr{F}_{i,A_{\mathscr{K}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},F^{\bullet}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},\iota)

where

•

s_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}\in S(A_{\mathscr{K}})
is a point extending
s_{\mathscr{K}}
,

•

(\overline{X},D)_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}:=(\overline{X},D)\times_{S}s_{\mathscr{K}},

•

(\mathscr{F}_{i,A_{\mathscr{K}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},F^{\bullet}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}})
is an isomonodromic flat bundle equipped with Griffiths-transverse filtration on
(\overline{X},D)_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}/s_{\mathscr{K}}
, and
\iota
is an isomorphism

\iota:(\mathscr{F}_{i,A_{\mathscr{K}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},F^{\bullet}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}})|_{\overline{X}}\simeq(\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F^{\bullet}).

Theorem 11.2.2

(Smoothness of deformation space)
.

For each
i
, the natural map

\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F^{\bullet}}\to\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}

defined by

(s_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},\mathscr{F}_{i,A_{\mathscr{K}}},\nabla_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},F^{\bullet}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},\iota)\mapsto s_{A_{\mathscr{K}}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It is straightforward to check that
\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F^{\bullet}}
is representable by a noetherian
\mathscr{K}
-algebra, say
\mathfrak{T}_{i}
, using that
(\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla)
is simple. We are studying a map

\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}},

and the latter is the spectrum of a smooth noetherian complete local
\mathscr{K}
-algebra
\mathfrak{R}
; we write
r_{i}:\mathfrak{R}\rightarrow\mathfrak{T}_{i}
for the corresponding map on algebras.
11
11

11

Recall from
§ 10.1
that
\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}
is the completion of
S_{\mathscr{K}}
at
s_{\mathscr{K}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{R}},\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{T}_{i}}
be the maximal ideals of
\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{T}_{i}
, respectively, and set
\mathfrak{R}_{n}=\mathfrak{R}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{n}
as in
§ 11.1
. As the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence degenerates for each
\mathscr{F}_{i}
, any deformation of
(\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla)
over
\mathscr{K}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
carries a unique Griffiths transverse filtration on an irreducible flat bundle if it exists, by
7.4.2
; that is, the map

\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F^{\bullet}}(\mathscr{K}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\rightarrow\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}(\mathscr{K}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

is injective. We deduce that
r_{i}
is a surjection on cotangent spaces
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{R}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{2}\to\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{T}_{i}}/\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{T}_{i}}^{2}
. It is hence surjective as a map of rings; let
I_{i}:=\ker(r_{i})
. We now prove by induction on
n
that
I_{i}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{n}
.

We will do this simultaneously for the
\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F^{\bullet}}
’s: that is, we assume that
I_{i}\subset\mathfrak{m}^{n}
for all
i
, and we aim to deduce
I_{i}\subset\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}
for all
i
using
11.1.1
, as we now spell out.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know that for each
i
the natural closed embedding
q_{n}:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n})\to\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}
factors through some (unique) map
\sigma_{i}:{\rm Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(\mathfrak{T}_{i})
. By
11.1.2
, it suffices to show that for any morphism
f:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\to\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}
such that the composition

\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n})\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\xrightarrow{f}\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}

is
q_{n}
, there exists
f_{\mathfrak{T}_{i}}:\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\to\mathfrak{T}_{i}
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(\mathfrak{T}_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathfrak{T}_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

commutes. In other words, let
(\mathscr{F}_{i,n},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
be the filtered flat bundle on
(\overline{X},D)_{\mathfrak{R}_{n}}
corresponding to
\sigma_{i}
; it remains to show that each
(\mathscr{F}_{i,n},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
deforms to
(\overline{X},D)_{\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}/\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
in an isomonodromic and Griffiths-transverse manner, where
(\overline{X},D)_{\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
denotes the fiber product
(\overline{X},D)\times_{\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}},f}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}).

Let

(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathscr{K}}},\nabla,F^{\bullet}):=\bigoplus(\mathscr{F}_{i,n},\nabla,F^{\bullet}).

We now set up the situation so that the assumptions of
§ 10.3.1
are satisfied. As in
§ 10.3.1
replace
R
with a finitely-generated localization so that
\widehat{S}\simeq R[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S}]]
; fix such a set of local coordinates. After replacing
R
with a further finitely-generated localization, we may assume that
f
and each
(\mathscr{F}_{i,n},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
descends to a Griffiths-transverse filtered flat bundle
(\mathscr{F}_{A,i},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
on
A=R[[t_{1},\cdots,t_{\dim S}]]/(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})^{n}
; note that
A
is cut out by a monomial ideal, so
10.3.2
is satisfied. The first part of
10.3.3
and
10.3.5
is immediate from our setup—set

(\mathscr{E}_{A},\nabla,F^{\bullet})=\bigoplus(\mathscr{F}_{A,i},\nabla,F^{\bullet})

with the induced connection and Griffiths-transverse filtration. The second part of
10.3.3
may be guaranteed by yet again replacing
R
by a finitely-generated localization. Finally, we make a choice of
F_{0}
as in
§ 10.3.1
so that
10.3.6
is satisfied.

We let
\mathfrak{p}
be a maximal ideal of
R
of sufficiently large residue characteristic, and, as in
§ 10.3.1
, let
\widehat{R}
be the completion of
R
at
\mathfrak{p}
. Note that e.g. by
10.3.6
we may assume that there exists a lift of Frobenius
F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}:\operatorname{Spf}(A_{\widehat{R}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Spf}(A_{\widehat{R}})
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spf}(A_{\widehat{R}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Spf}(A_{\widehat{R}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{A_{\widehat{R}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}

commutes. Note that this map factors through the restriction of
F_{0}
to
B_{\widehat{R}}
.

By
10.3.9
(1),
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
is a Higgs-de Rham fixed point, and hence the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence for
(\mathscr{E}_{A_{\mathfrak{p}}},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
degenerates by
11.3.1
below. Hence the same is true for
(\mathscr{E}_{A},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
and hence for each
(\mathscr{F}_{A,i},\nabla,F^{\bullet})
.

Now the result is immediate from
10.3.12
, which provides the required Griffiths-transverse extension of the Hodge filtration on
(\mathscr{F}_{i,n},\nabla)
to its isomonodromic deformation to
(\overline{X},D)_{\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}/\operatorname{Spec}(\mathfrak{R}_{n}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
, as desired.

∎

Proof of
Theorem 10.1.3
.

The proof is immediate from
Theorem 11.2.2
. Indeed, we’ve shown that for each
i
the natural map

\Def_{\mathscr{F}_{i},\nabla,F^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}},

is an isomorphism. The inverse of this map precisely yields a Griffiths-transverse deformation of the Hodge filtration on
\mathscr{F}_{i}
to its isomonodromic deformation to
\widehat{S}_{\mathscr{K}}
. Taking the direct sum of these filtered flat bundles yields the desired statement.
∎

11.3.
Hodge-de Rham degeneration for Higgs-de Rham fixed points

In this section we record a basic fact about Hodge-de Rham degeneration for Griffiths-transverse filtered flat bundles which are Higgs-de Rham fixed points, or in more classical language, Fontaine modules, that were used in the proof of
Theorem 11.2.2
above. These facts are surely well-known to experts but we were unable to find a suitable reference.

Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
, and
A
an Artin local
k
-algebra with residue field
k
. Suppose
\overline{f}:\overline{X}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A)
is a smooth projective morphism, and
D\subset\overline{X}
is a normal crossings divisor relative to
{\rm Spec}(A)
; we write
X
for
\overline{X}\setminus D
and
f:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A)
for the natural map. We form the Cartesian square

(11.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{X},D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(A).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\text{abs}}}

The following is a version of a result of Deligne–Illusie
[
DI87
, Prop 4.1.2]
for Higgs-de Rham fixed points:

Lemma 11.3.1
.

Suppose
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
is a flat bundle on
(\overline{X},D)/{\rm Spec}(A)
, and
F
a Griffiths transverse filtration and nilpotent residues along
D
; we denote by
(\gr_{F}\mathscr{E},\theta)
the associated graded Higgs bundle, which we assume lies in
\operatorname{HIG}_{p-1}(\overline{X},D)
.

Suppose we have a
W_{2}(k)
-lift
(\bar{X}_{W_{2}}^{\prime},D_{W_{2}}^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(\tilde{A})
of
(\bar{X}^{\prime},D^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A)
, which defines the inverse Cartier transform
C^{-1}
as in
§ 9.1
. Suppose the length of
s
is less than
p-\dim_{A}\overline{X}
.

Suppose
(\mathscr{E},\nabla,F)
is a Higgs-de Rham fixed point relative to this lift, in the sense that

(11.2)

C^{-1}G^{*}(\gr_{F}\mathscr{E},\theta)\simeq(\mathscr{E},\nabla).

Then the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence for
\mathscr{E}_{dR}

E_{1}^{p,q}=\mathbb{H}^{q}(\gr^{p}_{F}(\mathscr{E}_{dR}))\Longrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})

degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page, and the terms of the
E_{1}
-page are free
A
-modules, and the formation of
\mathbb{H}^{q}(\gr^{p}_{F}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})),\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})
commutes with arbitrary base change.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as the proof of
[
DI87
, Prop 4.1.2]
. We restrict ourselves to the case when
D
is empty and therefore
\overline{X}=X
; the general case is only notationally more complicated.

Let
\mathfrak{m}
be the maximal ideal of
A
, and
N
be the smallest integer such that
\mathfrak{m}^{N}=0
. We prove the proposition by induction on
N
. The base case
N=1
, i.e.
A=k
, follows immediately from
[
OV07
, Theorem 4.17]
(and from
[
KYZ20
, Lemma 6.1]
in general).
12
12

12

In our application, the filtered flat bundle in the base case comes from a geometric situation which lifts to
W_{2}(k)
, in which case this is due to Deligne-Illusie
[
DI87
]
.
Let
\iota:{\rm Spec}(k)\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A)
be the inclusion of the closed point, and write
\iota:X_{k}:=X\times_{{\rm Spec}(A)}{\rm Spec}(k)\rightarrow X
.
Let

h^{p,q}:=\dim_{k}\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\gr^{p}_{F}\iota^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})).

We now proceed with the induction step. The Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence (
11.2
) implies that

(11.3)

\lg\mathbb{H}^{p}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})\leq\sum_{p+q=n}\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\gr^{p}_{F}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})),

where
\lg
stands for the length; the spectral sequence degenerates if and only if this is an equality.

For each
p
, we may write
Rf_{*}(\gr^{p}(\mathscr{E}_{dR}))
as a bounded complex of finitely-generated flat, and hence free, modules. It follows from basechange that

(11.4)

\lg\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\gr^{p}_{F}(\mathscr{E}_{dR}))\leq h^{p,q}\lg A,

with equality if and only if the cohomology groups are free over
A
.
On the other hand, since
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
is a Higgs-de Rham fixed point, by
9.2.3
, the conjugate spectral sequence

E_{2}^{p,q}=\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(G^{*}\gr^{q}_{\text{conj}}(\mathscr{E}_{dR}))\Longrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})

degenerates, where we define the conjugate filtration using the inverse Cartier transform as in
10.3.7
; therefore

\lg\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})=\sum_{p+q=n}\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(G^{*}\gr^{q}_{\text{conj}}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})).

Now the absolute Frobenius on
A
factors as

{\rm Spec}(A)\rightarrow T\xhookrightarrow{j}{\rm Spec}(A)

where
j:T\xhookrightarrow{}{\rm Spec}(A)
is a proper closed subscheme. It follows by our inductive hypothesis that
\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(j^{*}\gr^{q}_{\text{conj}}(\mathscr{E}_{dR}))
are free over
T
of rank
h^{q,p}
. Hence by basechange
\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(G^{*}\gr^{q}_{\text{conj}}(\mathscr{E}_{dR}))
is free over
A
of rank
h^{q,p}
. Therefore

\lg\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})=\sum_{p+q=n}h^{p,q}\lg A.

Combining this with (
11.3
), (
11.4
), we deduce

(11.5)

\lg\mathbb{H}^{n}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})=\sum_{p+q=n}\mathbb{H}^{n}(\gr^{p}_{F}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})),

and hence the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence degenerates. Moreover, equality holds in (
11.4
), which means that the cohomology groups
\mathbb{H}^{n}(\gr^{p}_{F}(\mathscr{E}_{dR}))
are free, and hence the same is true for
\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(\mathscr{E}_{dR})
, thus completing the induction step.
∎

12.
Proof of main theorem

We now prove
Theorem 6.1.3
; it will, more or less, be an immediate consequence of
Theorem 10.1.3
after some simple reductions.

12.1.
Reduction to the Deligne canonical extension

The following lemma will be useful for us for the reduction to the case of nilpotent residues.

Lemma 12.1.1
.

Let
R\subset\mathbb{C}
be a finitely-generated
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra,
S
a smooth
R
-scheme,
s\in S(R)
, and
(\overline{X},D)
a smooth projective
S
-scheme equipped with a simple normal crossings divisor
D
over
S
. Let
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
be a flat bundle on
(\overline{X},D)_{s}
, and suppose it admits an (
\omega(p)
-integral) formal isomonodromic deformation over
\widehat{S}
, the completion of
S
at
s
. Suppose the eigenvalues of the residue matrices of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
along
D_{s}
are rational numbers.

Then there exists a flat bundle
(\mathscr{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
on
(\overline{X},D)_{s}
with
(\mathscr{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})|_{\overline{X}_{s}\setminus D_{s}}\simeq(\mathscr{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})|_{\overline{X}_{s}\setminus D_{s}}
and whose residue matrices have eigenvalues in
[0,1)
. Moreover
(\mathscr{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
has (
\omega(p)
-integral) formal isomonodromic deformation over
\widehat{S}
.

Proof.

The point of the proof is that one may modify
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
to along
D
to obtain the desired condition on residues; as one can do this integrally and in families, the claim about (
\omega(p)
-integral) formal isomonodromic deformation follows. We briefly explain how to perform this modification. Let
D_{i}=\cup D_{i}
, with the
D_{i}
irreducible.

First, we may without loss of generality assume all the eigenvalues of the residue matrices of
(\mathscr{E},\nabla)
are negative, by tensoring with
\mathscr{O}(ND)
for
N\gg 0
. Let
\alpha
be the most negative eigenvalue that appears, say along some component
D_{j}
of
D
, and consider the map

\mathscr{E}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathscr{E}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}_{s}}(\log D_{s})/\Omega^{1}_{\overline{X}_{s}}\simeq\bigoplus_{i}\mathscr{E}|_{D_{i}}\to\mathscr{E}|_{D_{j}}\xrightarrow{(\operatorname{Res}_{\nabla,D_{i}}-\alpha)^{r}}\mathscr{E}|_{D_{j}}

for
r
sufficiently large. Replacing
\mathscr{E}
by the kernel of this map has the effect of increasing
\alpha
by
1
. Iterating this procedure we may move all the eigenvalues of the residue matrices into
[0,1)
